      and Finances\n\nMore...\n\nHelp out\n\nGive\n\nSponsor\n\nAdvertise\n\nCreate\n\nPromote\n\nJoin
      Lawyer Directory\n\nLII\n\nElectronic Code of Federal Regulations (e-CFR)\n\nTitle
      31\u2014Money and Finance: Treasury\n\nSubtitle B\u2014Regulations Relating
      to Money and Finance\n\nCHAPTER X\u2014FINANCIAL CRIMES ENFORCEMENT NETWORK,
      DEPARTMENT OF THE TREASURY\n\nPART 1022\u2014RULES FOR MONEY SERVICES BUSINESSES\n\nSubpart
      B\u2014Programs\n\n\u00a7 1022.210 Anti-money laundering programs for money
      services businesses.\n\n31 CFR \u00a7 1022.210 - Anti-money laundering programs
      for money services businesses.\n\nCFR\n\nprev | next\n\n\u00a7 1022.210 Anti-money
      laundering programs for money services businesses.\n\n(a) Each money services
      business, as defined by \u00a7 1010.100(ff) of this chapter, shall develop,
      implement, and maintain an effective anti-money laundering program. An effective
      anti-money laundering program is one that is reasonably designed to prevent
      the money services business from being used to facilitate money laundering and
      the financing of terrorist activities.\n\n(b) The program shall be commensurate
      with the risks posed by the location and size of, and the nature and volume
      of the financial services provided by, the money services business.\n\n(c) The
      program shall be in writing, and a money services business shall make copies
      of the anti-money laundering program available for inspection to the Department
      of the Treasury upon request.\n\n(d) At a minimum, the program shall:\n\n(1)
      Incorporate policies, procedures, and internal controls reasonably designed
      to assure compliance with this chapter.\n\n(i) Policies, procedures, and internal
      controls developed and implemented under this section shall include provisions
      for complying with the requirements of this chapter including, to the extent
      applicable to the money services business, requirements for:\n\n(A) Verifying
      customer identification, including as set forth in paragraph (d)(1)(iv) of this
      section;\n\n(B) Filing Reports;\n\n(C) Creating and retaining records;\n\n(D)
      Responding to law enforcement requests.\n\n(ii) Money services businesses that
      have automated data processing systems should integrate their compliance procedures
      with such systems.\n\n(iii) A person that is a money services business solely
      because it is an agent for another money services business as set forth in \u00a7
      1022.380(a)(3) , and the money services business for which it serves as agent,
      may by agreement allocate between them responsibility for development of policies,
      procedures, and internal controls required by this paragraph (d)(1). Each money
      services business shall remain solely responsible for implementation of the
      requirements set forth in this section, and nothing in this paragraph (d)(1)
      relieves any money services business from its obligation to establish and maintain
      an effective anti-money laundering program.\n\n(iv) A money services business
      that is a provider or seller of prepaid access must establish procedures to
      verify the identity of a person who obtains prepaid access under a prepaid program
      and obtain identifying information concerning such a person , including name,
      date of birth, address, and identification number. Sellers of prepaid access
      must also establish procedures to verify the identity of a person who obtains
      prepaid access to funds that exceed $10,000 during any one day and obtain identifying
      information concerning such a person , including name, date of birth, address,
      and identification number. Providers of prepaid access must retain access to
      such identifying information for five years after the last use of the prepaid
      access device or vehicle; such information obtained by sellers of prepaid access
      must be retained for five years from the date of the sale of the prepaid access
      device or vehicle.\n\n(2) Designate a person to assure day to day compliance
      with the program and this chapter. The responsibilities of such person shall
      include assuring that:\n\n(i) The money services business properly files reports,
      and creates and retains records, in accordance with applicable requirements
      of this chapter;\n\n(ii) The compliance program is updated as necessary to reflect
      current requirements of this chapter, and related guidance issued by the Department
      of the Treasury ; and\n\n(iii) The money services business provides appropriate
      training and education in accordance with paragraph (d)(3) of this section.\n\n(3)
      Provide education and/or training of appropriate personnel concerning their
      responsibilities under the program, including training in the detection of suspicious
      transactions to the extent that the money services business is required to report
      such transactions under this chapter.\n\n(4) Provide for independent review
      to monitor and maintain an adequate program. The scope and frequency of the
      review shall be commensurate with the risk of the financial services provided
      by the money services business. Such review may be conducted by an officer or
      employee of the money services business so long as the reviewer is not the person
      designated in paragraph (d)(2) of this section.", "has_more_in_unit": true,
      "paragraph_continues": true, "next_read": {"source_id": "SRC-cb07ecbed7d046506d81",

      "start": 0, "end": 5287, "page_number": null, "section_label": "Section 1",
      "extraction_method": "text", "text": "31 CFR \u00a7 1022.320 - Reports by money
      services businesses of suspicious transactions. | Electronic Code of Federal
      Regulations (e-CFR) | US Law | LII / Legal Information Institute\n\nPlease help
      us improve our site!\n\n\u00d7\n\nNo thank you\n\nSkip to main content\n\nCornell

      Lawyer Directory\n\nLII\n\nElectronic Code of Federal Regulations (e-CFR)\n\nTitle
      31\u2014Money and Finance: Treasury\n\nSubtitle B\u2014Regulations Relating
      to Money and Finance\n\nCHAPTER X\u2014FINANCIAL CRIMES ENFORCEMENT NETWORK,
      DEPARTMENT OF THE TREASURY\n\nPART 1022\u2014RULES FOR MONEY SERVICES BUSINESSES\n\nSubpart
      C\u2014Reports Required To Be Made By Money Services Businesses\n\n\u00a7 1022.320
      Reports by money services businesses of suspicious transactions.\n\n31 CFR \u00a7
      1022.320 - Reports by money services businesses of suspicious transactions.\n\nCFR\n\nTable
      of Popular Names\n\nprev | next\n\n\u00a7 1022.320 Reports by money services
      businesses of suspicious transactions.\n\n(a)  General.\n\n(1) Every money services
      business described in \u00a7 1010.100(ff)(1) , (3), (4), (5), (6), and (7) of
      this chapter, shall file with the Treasury Department, to the extent and in
      the manner required by this section, a report of any suspicious transaction
      relevant to a possible violation of law or regulation. Any money services business
      may also file with the Treasury Department, by using the form specified in paragraph
      (b)(1) of this section, or otherwise, a report of any suspicious transaction
      that it believes is relevant to the possible violation of any law or regulation
      but whose reporting is not required by this section.\n\n(2) A transaction requires
      reporting under the terms of this section if it is conducted or attempted by,
      at, or through a money services business, involves or aggregates funds or other
      assets of at least $2,000 (except as provided in paragraph (a)(3) of this section),
      and the money services business knows, suspects, or has reason to suspect that
      the transaction (or a pattern of transactions of which the transaction is a
      part):\n\n(i) Involves funds derived from illegal activity or is intended or
      conducted in order to hide or disguise funds or assets derived from illegal
      activity (including, without limitation, the ownership, nature, source, location,
      or control of such funds or assets) as part of a plan to violate or evade any
      Federal law or regulation or to avoid any transaction reporting requirement
      under Federal law or regulation;\n\n(ii) Is designed, whether through structuring
      or other means, to evade any requirements of this chapter or of any other regulations
      promulgated under the Bank Secrecy Act ; or\n\n(iii) Serves no business or apparent
      lawful purpose, and the reporting money services business knows of no reasonable
      explanation for the transaction after examining the available facts, including
      the background and possible purpose of the transaction .\n\n(iv) Involves use
      of the money services business to facilitate criminal activity.\n\n(3) To the
      extent that the identification of transactions required to be reported is derived
      from a review of clearance records or other similar records of money orders
      or traveler''s checks that have been sold or processed, an issuer of money orders
      or traveler''s checks shall only be required to report a transaction or pattern
      of transactions that involves or aggregates funds or other assets of at least
      $5,000.\n\n(4) The obligation to identify and properly and timely to report
      a suspicious transaction rests with each money services business involved in
      the transaction , provided that no more than one report is required to be filed
      by the money services businesses involved in a particular transaction (so long
      as the report filed contains all relevant facts). Whether, in addition to any
      liability on its own for failure to report, a money services business that issues
      the instrument or provides the funds transfer service involved in the transaction
      may be liable for the failure of another money services business involved in
      the transaction to report that transaction depends upon the nature of the contractual
      or other relationship between the businesses, and the legal effect of the facts
      and circumstances of the relationship and transaction involved, under general
      principles of the law of agency.\n\n(b)  Filing procedures \u2014(1) What to
      file. A suspicious transaction shall be reported by completing a Suspicious
      Activity Report(\u201cSAR\u201d), and collecting and maintaining supporting
      documentation as required by paragraph (c) of this section.\n\n(2)  Where to
      file. The SAR shall be filed in a central location to be determined by FinCEN
      , as indicated in the instructions to the SAR.", "has_more_in_unit": true, "paragraph_continues":
      true, "next_read": {"source_id": "SRC-0733e479993b0c9d3063", "source_version":

      "warnings": [], "remaining_budget": {"main_calls": 9, "batches": 3, "requests":
      11, "fetches": 16, "active_seconds": 107.4090747910086, "evidence_chars": 22663}}}'

      31\u2014Money and Finance: Treasury\n\nSubtitle B\u2014Regulations Relating
      to Money and Finance\n\nCHAPTER X\u2014FINANCIAL CRIMES ENFORCEMENT NETWORK,
      DEPARTMENT OF THE TREASURY\n\nPART 1010\u2014GENERAL PROVISIONS\n\nSubpart D\u2014Records
      Required To Be Maintained\n\n\u00a7 1010.430 Nature of records and retention
      period.\n\n31 CFR \u00a7 1010.430 - Nature of records and retention period.\n\nCFR\n\nprev
      | next\n\n\u00a7 1010.430 Nature of records and retention period.\n\n(a) Wherever
      it is required that there be retained either the original or a copy or reproduction
      of a check, draft, monetary instrument , investment security , or other similar
      instrument, there shall be retained a copy of both front and back of each such
      instrument or document, except that no copy need be retained of the back of
      any instrument or document which is entirely blank or which contains only standardized
      printed information, a copy of which is on file.\n\n(b) Records required by
      this chapter to be retained by financial institutions may be those made in the
      ordinary course of business by a financial institution . If no record is made
      in the ordinary course of business of any transaction with respect to which
      records are required to be retained by this chapter, then such a record shall
      be prepared in writing by the financial institution .\n\n(c) The rules and regulations
      issued by the Internal Revenue Service under 26 U.S.C. 6109 determine what constitutes
      a taxpayer identification number and whose number shall be obtained in the case
      of an account maintained by one or more persons.\n\n(d) All records that are
      required to be retained by this chapter shall be retained for a period of five
      years. Records or reports required to be kept pursuant to an order issued under
      \u00a7 1010.370 of this chapter shall be retained for the period of time specified
      in such order, not to exceed five years. All such records shall be filed or
      stored in such a way as to be accessible within a reasonable period of time,
      taking into consideration the nature of the record, and the amount of time expired
      since the record was made.\n\n[ 75 FR 65812 , Oct. 26, 2010, as amended at 81

        Title 31—Money and Finance: Treasury


        Subtitle B—Regulations Relating to Money and Finance


        CHAPTER X—FINANCIAL CRIMES ENFORCEMENT NETWORK, DEPARTMENT OF THE TREASURY


        PART 1022—RULES FOR MONEY SERVICES BUSINESSES


        Subpart B—Programs


        § 1022.210 Anti-money laundering programs for money services businesses.


        31 CFR § 1022.210 - Anti-money laundering programs for money services businesses.


        CFR


        prev | next


        § 1022.210 Anti-money laundering programs for money services businesses.


        (a) Each money services business, as defined by § 1010.100(ff) of this chapter,
        shall develop, implement, and maintain an effective anti-money laundering
        program. An effective anti-money laundering program is one that is reasonably
        designed to prevent the money services business from being used to facilitate
        money laundering and the financing of terrorist activities.


        (b) The program shall be commensurate with the risks posed by the location
        and size of, and the nature and volume of the financial services provided
        by, the money services business.


        (c) The program shall be in writing, and a money services business shall make
        copies of the anti-money laundering program available for inspection to the
        Department of the Treasury upon request.


        (d) At a minimum, the program shall:


        (1) Incorporate policies, procedures, and internal controls reasonably designed
        to assure compliance with this chapter.


        (i) Policies, procedures, and internal controls developed and implemented
        under this section shall include provisions for complying with the requirements
        of this chapter including, to the extent applicable to the money services
        business, requirements for:


        (A) Verifying customer identification, including as set forth in paragraph
        (d)(1)(iv) of this section;


        (B) Filing Reports;


        (C) Creating and retaining records;


        (D) Responding to law enforcement requests.


        (ii) Money services businesses that have automated data processing systems
        should integrate their compliance procedures with such systems.


        (iii) A person that is a money services business solely because it is an agent
        for another money services business as set forth in § 1022.380(a)(3) , and
        the money services business for which it serves as agent, may by agreement
        allocate between them responsibility for development of policies, procedures,
        and internal controls required by this paragraph (d)(1). Each money services
        business shall remain solely responsible for implementation of the requirements
        set forth in this section, and nothing in this paragraph (d)(1) relieves any
        money services business from its obligation to establish and maintain an effective
        anti-money laundering program.


        (iv) A money services business that is a provider or seller of prepaid access
        must establish procedures to verify the identity of a person who obtains prepaid
        access under a prepaid program and obtain identifying information concerning
        such a person , including name, date of birth, address, and identification
        number. Sellers of prepaid access must also establish procedures to verify
        the identity of a person who obtains prepaid access to funds that exceed $10,000
        during any one day and obtain identifying information concerning such a person
        , including name, date of birth, address, and identification number. Providers
        of prepaid access must retain access to such identifying information for five
        years after the last use of the prepaid access device or vehicle; such information
        obtained by sellers of prepaid access must be retained for five years from
        the date of the sale of the prepaid access device or vehicle.


        (2) Designate a person to assure day to day compliance with the program and
        this chapter. The responsibilities of such person shall include assuring that:


        (i) The money services business properly files reports, and creates and retains
        records, in accordance with applicable requirements of this chapter;


        (ii) The compliance program is updated as necessary to reflect current requirements
        of this chapter, and related guidance issued by the Department of the Treasury
        ; and


        (iii) The money services business provides appropriate training and education
        in accordance with paragraph (d)(3) of this section.


        (3) Provide education and/or training of appropriate personnel concerning
        their responsibilities under the program, including training in the detection
        of suspicious transactions to the extent that the money services business
        is required to report such transactions under this chapter.


        (4) Provide for independent review to monitor and maintain an adequate program.
        The scope and frequency of the review shall be commensurate with the risk
        of the financial services provided by the money services business. Such review
        may be conducted by an officer or employee of the money services business
        so long as the reviewer is not the person designated in paragraph (d)(2) of
        this section.'

        Title 31—Money and Finance: Treasury


        Subtitle B—Regulations Relating to Money and Finance


        CHAPTER X—FINANCIAL CRIMES ENFORCEMENT NETWORK, DEPARTMENT OF THE TREASURY


        PART 1022—RULES FOR MONEY SERVICES BUSINESSES


        Subpart C—Reports Required To Be Made By Money Services Businesses


        § 1022.320 Reports by money services businesses of suspicious transactions.


        31 CFR § 1022.320 - Reports by money services businesses of suspicious transactions.


        CFR


        Table of Popular Names


        prev | next


        § 1022.320 Reports by money services businesses of suspicious transactions.


        (a)  General.


        (1) Every money services business described in § 1010.100(ff)(1) , (3), (4),
        (5), (6), and (7) of this chapter, shall file with the Treasury Department,
        to the extent and in the manner required by this section, a report of any
        suspicious transaction relevant to a possible violation of law or regulation.
        Any money services business may also file with the Treasury Department, by
        using the form specified in paragraph (b)(1) of this section, or otherwise,
        a report of any suspicious transaction that it believes is relevant to the
        possible violation of any law or regulation but whose reporting is not required
        by this section.


        (2) A transaction requires reporting under the terms of this section if it
        is conducted or attempted by, at, or through a money services business, involves
        or aggregates funds or other assets of at least $2,000 (except as provided
        in paragraph (a)(3) of this section), and the money services business knows,
        suspects, or has reason to suspect that the transaction (or a pattern of transactions
        of which the transaction is a part):


        (i) Involves funds derived from illegal activity or is intended or conducted
        in order to hide or disguise funds or assets derived from illegal activity
        (including, without limitation, the ownership, nature, source, location, or
        control of such funds or assets) as part of a plan to violate or evade any
        Federal law or regulation or to avoid any transaction reporting requirement
        under Federal law or regulation;


        (ii) Is designed, whether through structuring or other means, to evade any
        requirements of this chapter or of any other regulations promulgated under
        the Bank Secrecy Act ; or


        (iii) Serves no business or apparent lawful purpose, and the reporting money
        services business knows of no reasonable explanation for the transaction after
        examining the available facts, including the background and possible purpose
        of the transaction .


        (iv) Involves use of the money services business to facilitate criminal activity.


        (3) To the extent that the identification of transactions required to be reported
        is derived from a review of clearance records or other similar records of
        money orders or traveler''s checks that have been sold or processed, an issuer
        of money orders or traveler''s checks shall only be required to report a transaction
        or pattern of transactions that involves or aggregates funds or other assets
        of at least $5,000.


        (4) The obligation to identify and properly and timely to report a suspicious
        transaction rests with each money services business involved in the transaction
        , provided that no more than one report is required to be filed by the money
        services businesses involved in a particular transaction (so long as the report
        filed contains all relevant facts). Whether, in addition to any liability
        on its own for failure to report, a money services business that issues the
        instrument or provides the funds transfer service involved in the transaction
        may be liable for the failure of another money services business involved
        in the transaction to report that transaction depends upon the nature of the
        contractual or other relationship between the businesses, and the legal effect
        of the facts and circumstances of the relationship and transaction involved,
        under general principles of the law of agency.


        (b)  Filing procedures —(1) What to file. A suspicious transaction shall be
        reported by completing a Suspicious Activity Report(“SAR”), and collecting
        and maintaining supporting documentation as required by paragraph (c) of this
        section.


        (2)  Where to file. The SAR shall be filed in a central location to be determined
        by FinCEN , as indicated in the instructions to the SAR.'

        Title 31—Money and Finance: Treasury


        Subtitle B—Regulations Relating to Money and Finance


        CHAPTER X—FINANCIAL CRIMES ENFORCEMENT NETWORK, DEPARTMENT OF THE TREASURY


        PART 1010—GENERAL PROVISIONS


        Subpart D—Records Required To Be Maintained


        § 1010.430 Nature of records and retention period.


        31 CFR § 1010.430 - Nature of records and retention period.


        CFR


        prev | next


        § 1010.430 Nature of records and retention period.


        (a) Wherever it is required that there be retained either the original or
        a copy or reproduction of a check, draft, monetary instrument , investment
        security , or other similar instrument, there shall be retained a copy of
        both front and back of each such instrument or document, except that no copy
        need be retained of the back of any instrument or document which is entirely
        blank or which contains only standardized printed information, a copy of which
        is on file.


        (b) Records required by this chapter to be retained by financial institutions
        may be those made in the ordinary course of business by a financial institution
        . If no record is made in the ordinary course of business of any transaction
        with respect to which records are required to be retained by this chapter,
        then such a record shall be prepared in writing by the financial institution
        .


        (c) The rules and regulations issued by the Internal Revenue Service under
        26 U.S.C. 6109 determine what constitutes a taxpayer identification number
        and whose number shall be obtained in the case of an account maintained by
        one or more persons.


        (d) All records that are required to be retained by this chapter shall be
        retained for a period of five years. Records or reports required to be kept
        pursuant to an order issued under § 1010.370 of this chapter shall be retained
        for the period of time specified in such order, not to exceed five years.
        All such records shall be filed or stored in such a way as to be accessible
        within a reasonable period of time, taking into consideration the nature of
        the record, and the amount of time expired since the record was made.


        [ 75 FR 65812 , Oct. 26, 2010, as amended at 81 FR 76864 , Nov. 4, 2016]

  - source_id: SRC-LOCAL-183892d6ee231d97ef4c
    source_hash: 73e298dedf82a3cd3cd03c2de42b05a91271b6008cfa33f0d1177846a54233f5
    source_version: 73e298dedf82a3cd3cd03c2de42b05a91271b6008cfa33f0d1177846a54233f5
    path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/sources/SRC-LOCAL-183892d6ee231d97ef4c.md
    original_path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/request.md
    title: request.md
    support_state: supplied
    source_type: workspace
    available_excerpt: '# Original Request


      From: Dana, PM — Growth & Integration

      Subject: Harbor migration — anything here need legal?


      We''re acquiring Harbor Pay. Close is targeted Nov 15 (~10 weeks). Migration
      plan:


      240k consumer users, 12k business accounts move into our app on day 1 post-close.
      One unified account, one balance, full access to our product set.

      No re-verification — they were already KYC''d by Harbor.

      Harbor holds MTLs in 38 states, we hold 41. Combined that''s 44 — nice win,
      opens four new markets.

      Harbor''s KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024. We have pass/fail
      results for everyone, but not the underlying documents for users onboarded before
      2023.

      Harbor''s monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract terminates at close.
      Plan is to repoint their transaction feed at our engine on day 1.

      Marketing wants a "Welcome" campaign with a $50 bonus on first transfer, launching
      close+1.

      Diligence flagged that Harbor has ~40 open alerts and "a handful" of SARs from
      the last year. Nobody''s read them yet.


      Does any of this need legal before close? It''s really just a data migration.

      '
    selected_passages:
    - start: 0
      end: 1095
      text: '# Original Request


        From: Dana, PM — Growth & Integration

        Subject: Harbor migration — anything here need legal?


        We''re acquiring Harbor Pay. Close is targeted Nov 15 (~10 weeks). Migration
        plan:


        240k consumer users, 12k business accounts move into our app on day 1 post-close.
        One unified account, one balance, full access to our product set.

        No re-verification — they were already KYC''d by Harbor.

        Harbor holds MTLs in 38 states, we hold 41. Combined that''s 44 — nice win,
        opens four new markets.

        Harbor''s KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024. We have pass/fail
        results for everyone, but not the underlying documents for users onboarded
        before 2023.

        Harbor''s monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract terminates at close.
        Plan is to repoint their transaction feed at our engine on day 1.

        Marketing wants a "Welcome" campaign with a $50 bonus on first transfer, launching
        close+1.

        Diligence flagged that Harbor has ~40 open alerts and "a handful" of SARs
        from the last year. Nobody''s read them yet.


        Does any of this need legal before close? It''s really just a data migration.

        '

    3187b3851135a12175e4228632e70533c21b345e49da78cd78033a52cfc68dc9:
      summary: Read 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/request.md.
      data:
        path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/request.md
        name: request.md
        content: '# Original Request


          From: Dana, PM — Growth & Integration

          Subject: Harbor migration — anything here need legal?


          We''re acquiring Harbor Pay. Close is targeted Nov 15 (~10 weeks). Migration
          plan:


          240k consumer users, 12k business accounts move into our app on day 1 post-close.
          One unified account, one balance, full access to our product set.

          No re-verification — they were already KYC''d by Harbor.

          Harbor holds MTLs in 38 states, we hold 41. Combined that''s 44 — nice win,
          opens four new markets.

          Harbor''s KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024. We have pass/fail
          results for everyone, but not the underlying documents for users onboarded
          before 2023.

          Harbor''s monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract terminates at
          close. Plan is to repoint their transaction feed at our engine on day 1.

          Marketing wants a "Welcome" campaign with a $50 bonus on first transfer,
          launching close+1.

          Diligence flagged that Harbor has ~40 open alerts and "a handful" of SARs
          from the last year. Nobody''s read them yet.


          Does any of this need legal before close? It''s really just a data migration.

          '
        metadata:
          request_id: REQ-20260909-283892
          matter_id: MAT-20260909-d89ad8
          original_text_preserved: true
          immutable: true

          Title 31—Money and Finance: Treasury


          Subtitle B—Regulations Relating to Money and Finance


          CHAPTER X—FINANCIAL CRIMES ENFORCEMENT NETWORK, DEPARTMENT OF THE TREASURY


          PART 1022—RULES FOR MONEY SERVICES BUSINESSES


          Subpart B—Programs


          § 1022.210 Anti-money laundering programs for money services businesses.


          31 CFR § 1022.210 - Anti-money laundering programs for money services businesses.


          CFR


          prev | next


          § 1022.210 Anti-money laundering programs for money services businesses.


          (a) Each money services business, as defined by § 1010.100(ff) of this chapter,
          shall develop, implement, and maintain an effective anti-money laundering
          program. An effective anti-money laundering program is one that is reasonably
          designed to prevent the money services business from being used to facilitate
          money laundering and the financing of terrorist activities.


          (b) The program shall be commensurate with the risks posed by the location
          and size of, and the nature and volume of the financial services provided
          by, the money services business.


          (c) The program shall be in writing, and a money services business shall
          make copies of the anti-money laundering program available for inspection
          to the Department of the Treasury upon request.


          (d) At a minimum, the program shall:


          (1) Incorporate policies, procedures, and internal controls reasonably designed
          to assure compliance with this chapter.


          (i) Policies, procedures, and internal controls developed and implemented
          under this section shall include provisions for complying with the requirements
          of this chapter including, to the extent applicable to the money services
          business, requirements for:


          (A) Verifying customer identification, including as set forth in paragraph
          (d)(1)(iv) of this section;


          (B) Filing Reports;


          (C) Creating and retaining records;


          (D) Responding to law enforcement requests.


          (ii) Money services businesses that have automated data processing systems
          should integrate their compliance procedures with such systems.


          (iii) A person that is a money services business solely because it is an
          agent for another money services business as set forth in § 1022.380(a)(3)
          , and the money services business for which it serves as agent, may by agreement
          allocate between them responsibility for development of policies, procedures,
          and internal controls required by this paragraph (d)(1). Each money services
          business shall remain solely responsible for implementation of the requirements
          set forth in this section, and nothing in this paragraph (d)(1) relieves
          any money services business from its obligation to establish and maintain
          an effective anti-money laundering program.


          (iv) A money services business that is a provider or seller of prepaid access
          must establish procedures to verify the identity of a person who obtains
          prepaid access under a prepaid program and obtain identifying information
          concerning such a person , including name, date of birth, address, and identification
          number. Sellers of prepaid access must also establish procedures to verify
          the identity of a person who obtains prepaid access to funds that exceed
          $10,000 during any one day and obtain identifying information concerning
          such a person , including name, date of birth, address, and identification
          number. Providers of prepaid access must retain access to such identifying
          information for five years after the last use of the prepaid access device
          or vehicle; such information obtained by sellers of prepaid access must
          be retained for five years from the date of the sale of the prepaid access
          device or vehicle.


          (2) Designate a person to assure day to day compliance with the program
          and this chapter. The responsibilities of such person shall include assuring
          that:


          (i) The money services business properly files reports, and creates and
          retains records, in accordance with applicable requirements of this chapter;


          (ii) The compliance program is updated as necessary to reflect current requirements
          of this chapter, and related guidance issued by the Department of the Treasury
          ; and


          (iii) The money services business provides appropriate training and education
          in accordance with paragraph (d)(3) of this section.


          (3) Provide education and/or training of appropriate personnel concerning
          their responsibilities under the program, including training in the detection
          of suspicious transactions to the extent that the money services business
          is required to report such transactions under this chapter.


          (4) Provide for independent review to monitor and maintain an adequate program.
          The scope and frequency of the review shall be commensurate with the risk
          of the financial services provided by the money services business. Such
          review may be conducted by an officer or employee of the money services
          business so long as the reviewer is not the person designated in paragraph
          (d)(2) of this section.'

          Title 31—Money and Finance: Treasury


          Subtitle B—Regulations Relating to Money and Finance


          CHAPTER X—FINANCIAL CRIMES ENFORCEMENT NETWORK, DEPARTMENT OF THE TREASURY


          PART 1022—RULES FOR MONEY SERVICES BUSINESSES


          Subpart C—Reports Required To Be Made By Money Services Businesses


          § 1022.320 Reports by money services businesses of suspicious transactions.


          31 CFR § 1022.320 - Reports by money services businesses of suspicious transactions.


          CFR


          Table of Popular Names


          prev | next


          § 1022.320 Reports by money services businesses of suspicious transactions.


          (a)  General.


          (1) Every money services business described in § 1010.100(ff)(1) , (3),
          (4), (5), (6), and (7) of this chapter, shall file with the Treasury Department,
          to the extent and in the manner required by this section, a report of any
          suspicious transaction relevant to a possible violation of law or regulation.
          Any money services business may also file with the Treasury Department,
          by using the form specified in paragraph (b)(1) of this section, or otherwise,
          a report of any suspicious transaction that it believes is relevant to the
          possible violation of any law or regulation but whose reporting is not required
          by this section.


          (2) A transaction requires reporting under the terms of this section if
          it is conducted or attempted by, at, or through a money services business,
          involves or aggregates funds or other assets of at least $2,000 (except
          as provided in paragraph (a)(3) of this section), and the money services
          business knows, suspects, or has reason to suspect that the transaction
          (or a pattern of transactions of which the transaction is a part):


          (i) Involves funds derived from illegal activity or is intended or conducted
          in order to hide or disguise funds or assets derived from illegal activity
          (including, without limitation, the ownership, nature, source, location,
          or control of such funds or assets) as part of a plan to violate or evade
          any Federal law or regulation or to avoid any transaction reporting requirement
          under Federal law or regulation;


          (ii) Is designed, whether through structuring or other means, to evade any
          requirements of this chapter or of any other regulations promulgated under
          the Bank Secrecy Act ; or


          (iii) Serves no business or apparent lawful purpose, and the reporting money
          services business knows of no reasonable explanation for the transaction
          after examining the available facts, including the background and possible
          purpose of the transaction .


          (iv) Involves use of the money services business to facilitate criminal
          activity.


          (3) To the extent that the identification of transactions required to be
          reported is derived from a review of clearance records or other similar
          records of money orders or traveler''s checks that have been sold or processed,
          an issuer of money orders or traveler''s checks shall only be required to
          report a transaction or pattern of transactions that involves or aggregates
          funds or other assets of at least $5,000.


          (4) The obligation to identify and properly and timely to report a suspicious
          transaction rests with each money services business involved in the transaction
          , provided that no more than one report is required to be filed by the money
          services businesses involved in a particular transaction (so long as the
          report filed contains all relevant facts). Whether, in addition to any liability
          on its own for failure to report, a money services business that issues
          the instrument or provides the funds transfer service involved in the transaction
          may be liable for the failure of another money services business involved
          in the transaction to report that transaction depends upon the nature of
          the contractual or other relationship between the businesses, and the legal
          effect of the facts and circumstances of the relationship and transaction
          involved, under general principles of the law of agency.


          (b)  Filing procedures —(1) What to file. A suspicious transaction shall
          be reported by completing a Suspicious Activity Report(“SAR”), and collecting
          and maintaining supporting documentation as required by paragraph (c) of
          this section.


          (2)  Where to file. The SAR shall be filed in a central location to be determined
          by FinCEN , as indicated in the instructions to the SAR.'

          Title 31—Money and Finance: Treasury


          Subtitle B—Regulations Relating to Money and Finance


          CHAPTER X—FINANCIAL CRIMES ENFORCEMENT NETWORK, DEPARTMENT OF THE TREASURY


          PART 1010—GENERAL PROVISIONS


          Subpart D—Records Required To Be Maintained


          § 1010.430 Nature of records and retention period.


          31 CFR § 1010.430 - Nature of records and retention period.


          CFR


          prev | next


          § 1010.430 Nature of records and retention period.


          (a) Wherever it is required that there be retained either the original or
          a copy or reproduction of a check, draft, monetary instrument , investment
          security , or other similar instrument, there shall be retained a copy of
          both front and back of each such instrument or document, except that no
          copy need be retained of the back of any instrument or document which is
          entirely blank or which contains only standardized printed information,
          a copy of which is on file.


          (b) Records required by this chapter to be retained by financial institutions
          may be those made in the ordinary course of business by a financial institution
          . If no record is made in the ordinary course of business of any transaction
          with respect to which records are required to be retained by this chapter,
          then such a record shall be prepared in writing by the financial institution
          .


          (c) The rules and regulations issued by the Internal Revenue Service under
          26 U.S.C. 6109 determine what constitutes a taxpayer identification number
          and whose number shall be obtained in the case of an account maintained
          by one or more persons.


          (d) All records that are required to be retained by this chapter shall be
          retained for a period of five years. Records or reports required to be kept
          pursuant to an order issued under § 1010.370 of this chapter shall be retained
          for the period of time specified in such order, not to exceed five years.
          All such records shall be filed or stored in such a way as to be accessible
          within a reasonable period of time, taking into consideration the nature
          of the record, and the amount of time expired since the record was made.


          [ 75 FR 65812 , Oct. 26, 2010, as amended at 81 FR 76864 , Nov. 4, 2016]

        Title 31—Money and Finance: Treasury


        Subtitle B—Regulations Relating to Money and Finance


        CHAPTER X—FINANCIAL CRIMES ENFORCEMENT NETWORK, DEPARTMENT OF THE TREASURY


        PART 1022—RULES FOR MONEY SERVICES BUSINESSES


        Subpart B—Programs


        § 1022.210 Anti-money laundering programs for money services businesses.


        31 CFR § 1022.210 - Anti-money laundering programs for money services businesses.


        CFR


        prev | next


        § 1022.210 Anti-money laundering programs for money services businesses.


        (a) Each money services business, as defined by § 1010.100(ff) of this chapter,
        shall develop, implement, and maintain an effective anti-money laundering
        program. An effective anti-money laundering program is one that is reasonably
        designed to prevent the money services business from being used to facilitate
        money laundering and the financing of terrorist activities.


        (b) The program shall be commensurate with the risks posed by the location
        and size of, and the nature and volume of the financial services provided
        by, the money services business.


        (c) The program shall be in writing, and a money services business shall make
        copies of the anti-money laundering program available for inspection to the
        Department of the Treasury upon request.


        (d) At a minimum, the program shall:


        (1) Incorporate policies, procedures, and internal controls reasonably designed
        to assure compliance with this chapter.


        (i) Policies, procedures, and internal controls developed and implemented
        under this section shall include provisions for complying with the requirements
        of this chapter including, to the extent applicable to the money services
        business, requirements for:


        (A) Verifying customer identification, including as set forth in paragraph
        (d)(1)(iv) of this section;


        (B) Filing Reports;


        (C) Creating and retaining records;


        (D) Responding to law enforcement requests.


        (ii) Money services businesses that have automated data processing systems
        should integrate their compliance procedures with such systems.


        (iii) A person that is a money services business solely because it is an agent
        for another money services business as set forth in § 1022.380(a)(3) , and
        the money services business for which it serves as agent, may by agreement
        allocate between them responsibility for development of policies, procedures,
        and internal controls required by this paragraph (d)(1). Each money services
        business shall remain solely responsible for implementation of the requirements
        set forth in this section, and nothing in this paragraph (d)(1) relieves any
        money services business from its obligation to establish and maintain an effective
        anti-money laundering program.


        (iv) A money services business that is a provider or seller of prepaid access
        must establish procedures to verify the identity of a person who obtains prepaid
        access under a prepaid program and obtain identifying information concerning
        such a person , including name, date of birth, address, and identification
        number. Sellers of prepaid access must also establish procedures to verify
        the identity of a person who obtains prepaid access to funds that exceed $10,000
        during any one day and obtain identifying information concerning such a person
        , including name, date of birth, address, and identification number. Providers
        of prepaid access must retain access to such identifying information for five
        years after the last use of the prepaid access device or vehicle; such information
        obtained by sellers of prepaid access must be retained for five years from
        the date of the sale of the prepaid access device or vehicle.


        (2) Designate a person to assure day to day compliance with the program and
        this chapter. The responsibilities of such person shall include assuring that:


        (i) The money services business properly files reports, and creates and retains
        records, in accordance with applicable requirements of this chapter;


        (ii) The compliance program is updated as necessary to reflect current requirements
        of this chapter, and related guidance issued by the Department of the Treasury
        ; and


        (iii) The money services business provides appropriate training and education
        in accordance with paragraph (d)(3) of this section.


        (3) Provide education and/or training of appropriate personnel concerning
        their responsibilities under the program, including training in the detection
        of suspicious transactions to the extent that the money services business
        is required to report such transactions under this chapter.


        (4) Provide for independent review to monitor and maintain an adequate program.
        The scope and frequency of the review shall be commensurate with the risk
        of the financial services provided by the money services business. Such review
        may be conducted by an officer or employee of the money services business
        so long as the reviewer is not the person designated in paragraph (d)(2) of
        this section.'

        Title 31—Money and Finance: Treasury


        Subtitle B—Regulations Relating to Money and Finance


        CHAPTER X—FINANCIAL CRIMES ENFORCEMENT NETWORK, DEPARTMENT OF THE TREASURY


        PART 1022—RULES FOR MONEY SERVICES BUSINESSES


        Subpart C—Reports Required To Be Made By Money Services Businesses


        § 1022.320 Reports by money services businesses of suspicious transactions.


        31 CFR § 1022.320 - Reports by money services businesses of suspicious transactions.


        CFR


        Table of Popular Names


        prev | next


        § 1022.320 Reports by money services businesses of suspicious transactions.


        (a)  General.


        (1) Every money services business described in § 1010.100(ff)(1) , (3), (4),
        (5), (6), and (7) of this chapter, shall file with the Treasury Department,
        to the extent and in the manner required by this section, a report of any
        suspicious transaction relevant to a possible violation of law or regulation.
        Any money services business may also file with the Treasury Department, by
        using the form specified in paragraph (b)(1) of this section, or otherwise,
        a report of any suspicious transaction that it believes is relevant to the
        possible violation of any law or regulation but whose reporting is not required
        by this section.


        (2) A transaction requires reporting under the terms of this section if it
        is conducted or attempted by, at, or through a money services business, involves
        or aggregates funds or other assets of at least $2,000 (except as provided
        in paragraph (a)(3) of this section), and the money services business knows,
        suspects, or has reason to suspect that the transaction (or a pattern of transactions
        of which the transaction is a part):


        (i) Involves funds derived from illegal activity or is intended or conducted
        in order to hide or disguise funds or assets derived from illegal activity
        (including, without limitation, the ownership, nature, source, location, or
        control of such funds or assets) as part of a plan to violate or evade any
        Federal law or regulation or to avoid any transaction reporting requirement
        under Federal law or regulation;


        (ii) Is designed, whether through structuring or other means, to evade any
        requirements of this chapter or of any other regulations promulgated under
        the Bank Secrecy Act ; or


        (iii) Serves no business or apparent lawful purpose, and the reporting money
        services business knows of no reasonable explanation for the transaction after
        examining the available facts, including the background and possible purpose
        of the transaction .


        (iv) Involves use of the money services business to facilitate criminal activity.


        (3) To the extent that the identification of transactions required to be reported
        is derived from a review of clearance records or other similar records of
        money orders or traveler''s checks that have been sold or processed, an issuer
        of money orders or traveler''s checks shall only be required to report a transaction
        or pattern of transactions that involves or aggregates funds or other assets
        of at least $5,000.


        (4) The obligation to identify and properly and timely to report a suspicious
        transaction rests with each money services business involved in the transaction
        , provided that no more than one report is required to be filed by the money
        services businesses involved in a particular transaction (so long as the report
        filed contains all relevant facts). Whether, in addition to any liability
        on its own for failure to report, a money services business that issues the
        instrument or provides the funds transfer service involved in the transaction
        may be liable for the failure of another money services business involved
        in the transaction to report that transaction depends upon the nature of the
        contractual or other relationship between the businesses, and the legal effect
        of the facts and circumstances of the relationship and transaction involved,
        under general principles of the law of agency.


        (b)  Filing procedures —(1) What to file. A suspicious transaction shall be
        reported by completing a Suspicious Activity Report(“SAR”), and collecting
        and maintaining supporting documentation as required by paragraph (c) of this
        section.


        (2)  Where to file. The SAR shall be filed in a central location to be determined
        by FinCEN , as indicated in the instructions to the SAR.'

    matter_id: MAT-20260909-d89ad8
    captured_at: '2026-09-13T03:58:13.867645+00:00'
    inputs:
      business_question:
        question_id: BQ-c9d7c39f6380eb60
        text: Does any part of the Harbor Pay acquisition migration need legal review
          before the Nov 15 close?
        revision: legacy:6abf4a45ae87313af8db04b89d546e0e1a1c6137341b3d3c0d159c66cf64d54c
      facts:
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-182bcc
        text: Mosaic Relay is acquiring Harbor Pay; close targeted Nov 15 (~10 weeks
          out).
        source_ids:
        - REQ-20260909-283892
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-551367
        text: Migration moves 240k consumer users and 12k business accounts into Mosaic
          Relay's app on day 1 post-close, with one unified account and balance and
          full access to Mosaic's product set.
        source_ids:
        - REQ-20260909-283892
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-9a0c61
        text: Plan is no re-verification; Harbor users were already KYC'd by Harbor.
        source_ids:
        - REQ-20260909-283892
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-33293c
        text: Harbor holds money transmitter licenses (MTLs) in 38 states; Mosaic
          Relay holds 41; combined footprint is 44 states, opening four new markets.
        source_ids:
        - REQ-20260909-283892
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-aea8ec
        text: Harbor's KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024; Mosaic has
          pass/fail results for everyone but not the underlying documents for users
          onboarded before 2023.
        source_ids:
        - REQ-20260909-283892
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-073838
        text: Harbor's transaction monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract
          terminates at close; plan is to repoint Harbor's transaction feed at Mosaic's
          engine on day 1.
        source_ids:
        - REQ-20260909-283892
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-19aebc
        text: Marketing wants a 'Welcome' campaign with a $50 bonus on first transfer,
          launching close+1.
        source_ids:
        - REQ-20260909-283892
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-adf2cb
        text: Diligence flagged ~40 open alerts and 'a handful' of SARs from the last
          year at Harbor; nobody has read them yet.
        source_ids:
        - REQ-20260909-283892
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-4c1fd8
        text: Migration moves 240k consumer users and 12k business accounts into Mosaic
          Relay's app on day 1 post-close, with one unified account and balance.
        source_ids:
        - MSG-20260909-9835ba
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-f2c49f
        text: Plan is no re-verification of Harbor's existing KYC; Harbor users were
          already KYC'd.
        source_ids:
        - MSG-20260909-9835ba
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-13d4c1
        text: Harbor's KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024; pass/fail
          results exist for everyone but underlying documents are missing for users
          onboarded before 2023.
        source_ids:
        - MSG-20260909-9835ba
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-78d5cd
        text: Harbor's transaction monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract
          terminates at close; plan is to repoint the transaction feed to Mosaic Relay's
          engine on day 1.
        source_ids:
        - MSG-20260909-9835ba
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-9f7bde
        text: Diligence flagged ~40 open alerts and 'a handful' of SARs from the last
          year at Harbor; none have been read yet.
        source_ids:
        - MSG-20260909-9835ba
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-ed9f06
        text: How is the Harbor Pay acquisition structured? — Asset purchase (Mosaic
          buys Harbor's assets)
        source_ids:
        - MSG-20260909-2e549e
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-b5e71e
        text: Does Mosaic Relay's AML/CIP program currently permit relying on another
          institution's prior KYC without re-verification, or is re-verification required?

        source_ids:
        - MSG-20260909-2e549e
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-849b00
        text: What is known about Harbor's ~40 open alerts and the SARs from the last

        origin: null

      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-f0fb01

        source_ids:
        - MSG-20260909-2e549e
        origin: null

      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-620c62
        text: For the pre-2023 Harbor users with no underlying KYC documents, what

        - MSG-20260909-2e549e

      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-771ee1

        source_ids:
        - MSG-20260909-2e549e
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      assumptions:
      - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-29b823
        text: Mosaic Relay's existing money transmission licenses and Harbor's MTLs
          are transferable or can be maintained through the acquisition without new
          state approval
        reason: Needed to continue
      - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-3c5756
        text: The KYC pass/fail results are sufficient to satisfy Mosaic Relay's own
          BSA/AML program obligations without the underlying documents
        reason: Needed to continue
      - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-1a519f
        text: The transaction monitoring repoint can be completed and validated before
          close without a monitoring gap
        reason: Needed to continue
      - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-ca7dfd
        text: Harbor Pay is a licensed money transmitter subject to state MTL regimes
          and federal BSA/AML obligations (not yet confirmed).
        reason: Needed to continue
      - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-d40239
        text: Mosaic Relay is itself a licensed money transmitter in 41 states (per
          the request).
        reason: Needed to continue
      - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-57e86b
        text: The acquisition is structured as an asset or stock purchase, which affects
          whether Harbor's licenses and SAR/alert obligations transfer (structure
          not yet confirmed).
        reason: Needed to continue
      issues:
      - issue_id: ISS-c9d7c39f63-94e42e2b38090045
        title: 'Money transmission licensing: whether Mosaic Relay can operate under
          the combined 44-state footprint and whether Harbor''s MTLs transfer/survive
          the acquisition'
        why_it_matters: ''
        parent_issue_id: null
        fact_ids: []
        disposition: null
      - issue_id: ISS-c9d7c39f63-ca95e529feeb1045
        title: 'BSA/AML and KYC: whether relying on Harbor''s prior KYC (with missing
          underlying documents for pre-2023 users) satisfies Mosaic Relay''s independent
          CIP/AML obligations'
        why_it_matters: ''
        parent_issue_id: null
        fact_ids: []
        disposition: null
      - issue_id: ISS-c9d7c39f63-69af3f282d21ef38
        title: 'SAR and alert handling: obligations to review, file, and manage Harbor''s
          ~40 open alerts and SARs, and successor liability'
        why_it_matters: ''
        parent_issue_id: null
        fact_ids: []
        disposition: null
      - issue_id: ISS-c9d7c39f63-4825f0af7ec75542
        title: 'Monitoring vendor transition: continuity of transaction monitoring
          when the SaaS contract terminates at close'
        why_it_matters: ''
        parent_issue_id: null
        fact_ids: []
        disposition: null
      - issue_id: ISS-c9d7c39f63-72810d0a43d3ce66
        title: 'Marketing compliance: the $50 welcome bonus offer and any state money
          transmission or consumer protection implications'
        why_it_matters: ''
        parent_issue_id: null
        fact_ids: []
        disposition: null
      - issue_id: ISS-c9d7c39f63-af341445a7af4c06
        title: Customer account, contract, and data-transfer rights
        why_it_matters: An asset purchase does not by itself establish that customer
          accounts, contracts, balances, or personal data can move into Mosaic's app
          on day one.
        parent_issue_id: null
        fact_ids:
        - FACT-20260909-ed9f06
        - FACT-20260909-551367
        - FACT-20260909-4c1fd8
        disposition: null
      - issue_id: ISS-c9d7c39f63-d22ea3957f21252a
        title: Open alerts, suspicious activity history, and acquisition diligence
        why_it_matters: About 40 open alerts and several suspicious activity reports
          remain unreviewed. Their contents could affect the acquisition decision,
          deal protections, remediation, and account migration.
        parent_issue_id: null
        fact_ids: []
        disposition: null
      - issue_id: ISS-c9d7c39f63-65ae94508dcda1bf
        title: State money-transmission authority after the asset purchase
        why_it_matters: The migration plan assumes Harbor’s licenses will permit service
          in four new markets. An asset purchase might not transfer those licenses
          or their operating authority to Mosaic.
        parent_issue_id: null
        fact_ids: []
        disposition: null
      - issue_id: ISS-c9d7c39f63-30caa16a9234ccf2
        title: Reliance on Harbor’s prior identity verification
        why_it_matters: The plan would migrate 240,000 consumers and 12,000 business
          accounts without new verification. Harbor’s former vendor closed in 2024,
          and only pass or fail results are reported as available.
        parent_issue_id: null
        fact_ids: []
        disposition: null
      - issue_id: ISS-c9d7c39f63-1d13a6636b8fbe60
        title: Transaction-monitoring continuity at cutover
        why_it_matters: Harbor’s monitoring feed will move to Mosaic’s system when
          Harbor’s current service ends at close. An incomplete change could create
          a gap in required monitoring.
        parent_issue_id: null
        fact_ids: []
        disposition: null
      - issue_id: ISS-c9d7c39f63-29efc151650e76a2
        title: Purchase-agreement authority and risk allocation
        why_it_matters: Pre-close data transfers, testing, customer communications,
          or operating changes could conflict with transaction covenants, consent
          limits, closing conditions, or liability allocations.
        parent_issue_id: null
        fact_ids: []
        disposition: null
      - issue_id: ISS-c9d7c39f63-fcd3954739e05481
        title: Customer terms, privacy, data transfers, and welcome bonus
        why_it_matters: Migration could change the service provider, account access,
          available products, data recipients, and customer rights. The proposed $50
          bonus adds advertising and offer-term risk.
        parent_issue_id: null
        fact_ids: []
        disposition: null
      questions:
      - question_id: Q-SAR
        text: Have the ~40 open alerts and the handful of SARs from the last year
          been reviewed, and what did they reveal?
        consequence: Unread open alerts and SARs are the highest-risk item; they can
          create BSA/AML exposure that must be addressed before close and could affect
          the deal itself.
        state: answered
        answer: The open alerts and SAR history have not been reviewed and remain
          in diligence.
        answer_kind: reported_fact
        linked_fact_ids:
        - FACT-20260909-070aec
      - question_id: Q-KYC
        text: For the pre-2023 Harbor users with no underlying KYC documents, what
          is the plan to satisfy Mosaic Relay's own BSA/AML customer identification
          and recordkeeping obligations?
        consequence: Whether the missing underlying documents create a compliance
          gap depends on how Mosaic plans to satisfy its own CIP/recordkeeping obligations
          for these users.
        state: answered
        answer: It is not yet known whether Mosaic Relay's AML/CIP program permits
          reliance on Harbor's prior KYC; compliance must confirm.
        answer_kind: reported_fact
        linked_fact_ids:
        - FACT-20260909-620c62
      - question_id: Q-MTL
        text: For the four new markets opened by the combined license footprint, what
          is the plan for state money transmission licensing or approval before onboarding
          those customers?
        consequence: Whether the four new markets can be served at close depends on
          license transferability and state approval, which is a gating regulatory
          question.
        state: answered
        answer: Mosaic Relay currently plans to use or assume Harbor's licenses for
          the four new markets.
        answer_kind: reported_fact
        linked_fact_ids:
        - FACT-20260909-771ee1
      - question_id: Q-BONUS
        text: How is the $50 welcome bonus structured?
        consequence: The structure determines whether the bonus triggers consumer
          disclosure, funds-flow, or state promotional requirements.
        state: left_open
        answer: null
        answer_kind: null
        linked_fact_ids: []
      decisions: []
      files: {}

      source:SRC-LOCAL-183892d6ee231d97ef4c:
        kind: source
        record_id: SRC-LOCAL-183892d6ee231d97ef4c
        path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/sources/SRC-LOCAL-183892d6ee231d97ef4c.md
        revision: 73e298dedf82a3cd3cd03c2de42b05a91271b6008cfa33f0d1177846a54233f5
        file_revision: 73e298dedf82a3cd3cd03c2de42b05a91271b6008cfa33f0d1177846a54233f5
        source_class: supplied_source
        availability: selected_passage
        reported: false
        passages:
        - start: 0
          end: 1095
          text: '# Original Request


            From: Dana, PM — Growth & Integration

            Subject: Harbor migration — anything here need legal?


            We''re acquiring Harbor Pay. Close is targeted Nov 15 (~10 weeks). Migration
            plan:


            240k consumer users, 12k business accounts move into our app on day 1
            post-close. One unified account, one balance, full access to our product
            set.

            No re-verification — they were already KYC''d by Harbor.

            Harbor holds MTLs in 38 states, we hold 41. Combined that''s 44 — nice
            win, opens four new markets.

            Harbor''s KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024. We have pass/fail
            results for everyone, but not the underlying documents for users onboarded
            before 2023.

            Harbor''s monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract terminates at
            close. Plan is to repoint their transaction feed at our engine on day
            1.

            Marketing wants a "Welcome" campaign with a $50 bonus on first transfer,
            launching close+1.

            Diligence flagged that Harbor has ~40 open alerts and "a handful" of SARs
            from the last year. Nobody''s read them yet.


            Does any of this need legal before close? It''s really just a data migration.

            '
        source_version: 73e298dedf82a3cd3cd03c2de42b05a91271b6008cfa33f0d1177846a54233f5

            Title 31—Money and Finance: Treasury


            Subtitle B—Regulations Relating to Money and Finance


            CHAPTER X—FINANCIAL CRIMES ENFORCEMENT NETWORK, DEPARTMENT OF THE TREASURY


            PART 1022—RULES FOR MONEY SERVICES BUSINESSES


            Subpart B—Programs


            § 1022.210 Anti-money laundering programs for money services businesses.


            31 CFR § 1022.210 - Anti-money laundering programs for money services
            businesses.


            CFR


            prev | next


            § 1022.210 Anti-money laundering programs for money services businesses.


            (a) Each money services business, as defined by § 1010.100(ff) of this
            chapter, shall develop, implement, and maintain an effective anti-money
            laundering program. An effective anti-money laundering program is one
            that is reasonably designed to prevent the money services business from
            being used to facilitate money laundering and the financing of terrorist
            activities.


            (b) The program shall be commensurate with the risks posed by the location
            and size of, and the nature and volume of the financial services provided
            by, the money services business.


            (c) The program shall be in writing, and a money services business shall
            make copies of the anti-money laundering program available for inspection
            to the Department of the Treasury upon request.


            (d) At a minimum, the program shall:


            (1) Incorporate policies, procedures, and internal controls reasonably
            designed to assure compliance with this chapter.


            (i) Policies, procedures, and internal controls developed and implemented
            under this section shall include provisions for complying with the requirements
            of this chapter including, to the extent applicable to the money services
            business, requirements for:


            (A) Verifying customer identification, including as set forth in paragraph
            (d)(1)(iv) of this section;


            (B) Filing Reports;


            (C) Creating and retaining records;


            (D) Responding to law enforcement requests.


            (ii) Money services businesses that have automated data processing systems
            should integrate their compliance procedures with such systems.


            (iii) A person that is a money services business solely because it is
            an agent for another money services business as set forth in § 1022.380(a)(3)
            , and the money services business for which it serves as agent, may by
            agreement allocate between them responsibility for development of policies,
            procedures, and internal controls required by this paragraph (d)(1). Each
            money services business shall remain solely responsible for implementation
            of the requirements set forth in this section, and nothing in this paragraph
            (d)(1) relieves any money services business from its obligation to establish
            and maintain an effective anti-money laundering program.


            (iv) A money services business that is a provider or seller of prepaid
            access must establish procedures to verify the identity of a person who
            obtains prepaid access under a prepaid program and obtain identifying
            information concerning such a person , including name, date of birth,
            address, and identification number. Sellers of prepaid access must also
            establish procedures to verify the identity of a person who obtains prepaid
            access to funds that exceed $10,000 during any one day and obtain identifying
            information concerning such a person , including name, date of birth,
            address, and identification number. Providers of prepaid access must retain
            access to such identifying information for five years after the last use
            of the prepaid access device or vehicle; such information obtained by
            sellers of prepaid access must be retained for five years from the date
            of the sale of the prepaid access device or vehicle.


            (2) Designate a person to assure day to day compliance with the program
            and this chapter. The responsibilities of such person shall include assuring
            that:


            (i) The money services business properly files reports, and creates and
            retains records, in accordance with applicable requirements of this chapter;


            (ii) The compliance program is updated as necessary to reflect current
            requirements of this chapter, and related guidance issued by the Department
            of the Treasury ; and


            (iii) The money services business provides appropriate training and education
            in accordance with paragraph (d)(3) of this section.


            (3) Provide education and/or training of appropriate personnel concerning
            their responsibilities under the program, including training in the detection
            of suspicious transactions to the extent that the money services business
            is required to report such transactions under this chapter.


            (4) Provide for independent review to monitor and maintain an adequate
            program. The scope and frequency of the review shall be commensurate with
            the risk of the financial services provided by the money services business.
            Such review may be conducted by an officer or employee of the money services
            business so long as the reviewer is not the person designated in paragraph
            (d)(2) of this section.'
        source_version: fdb071b24ffb261142ba044fdda7e781
